Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `mac_int_18x19` (precision INT19)
- computes: acc += a*b
- input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
- output ports: out[37]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: on `clk`, the block registers its operands (`a`, `b`) at one clock edge and `out` at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in `out` after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. `out` is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and `reset` is its only control (no enable, no load).
- reset: `reset` is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
- power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by `reset`. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, spmv.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by reset. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified spmv under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 6.95433 ns on its one clock clk (I/O pads not timed) and uses 32 DSP tiles (the same at each of the five seeds). Its used logic-block area is 5.85603e+07 minimum-width transistor areas, so its area × delay, the objective, is 4.07248e+08. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in fetcher: no multiply, a register through six LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 5.96 ns in all, 1.60 of it into the third LUT; 7.089 ns on that report. The next slowest stage on the same report that starts at another register, in a module not established, is no multiply, a register through five LUTs into a flip-flop with enable; wire 5.51 ns in all, 6.376 ns, so a rewrite that shortens only the slowest stage leaves the period at about that; one that also shortens this stage can take it lower. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path. The block's own stage, input register to output register, 2.219 ns, is below the baseline's critical path.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: multiply.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products reach no sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: no sum.

bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$571no DSP tile; a product by a constant factor that synthesis turns into shifts, so the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when moving soft adders and flip-flops into blocks frees whole CLBs; a DSP tile costs the area of about 9 CLBs.
  • Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
Objective

Minimize area × delay against the original design. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or none expected to lower area × delay, or the best result so far is the best available. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).

A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_spmv.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

response

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 32
TILES: 16
STAGE_NS: 6.95433
MAPPING: per Channel instance (32 of them): clk -> block clk; mac_rst (new 1-bit input of Channel, driven from spmv as ~(start | start_d1)) -> block reset; {10'b0, vec_val} -> block a[18]; {11'b0, mat_val} -> block b[19]; block out[36:0] -> new wire mac_out, of which mac_out[15:0] is used; the value that used to be the register mult becomes the wire mult_din = mac_out[15:0] - mac_out_d, which drives fifo_mult.din. vec_val/mat_val are the (now combinational) douts of the per-channel mat_val FIFO in fetcher and vec_val FIFO in bvb.

PLAN:

Candidate regions

Converted: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (32 instances) — mult <= vec_val * mat_val (spmv.v:3317), the only real multiply in the design. In the baseline it sits one-to-a-tile on 32 DSP tiles in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode. 32 mac_int_18x19 blocks all driven by one clock and one shared reset net pack two to a tile, so the design goes from 32 DSP tiles to 16 — a saving of 16 × 253779 = 4.06e6 MWTA (~7% of the 5.856e7 baseline) against a small amount of added soft logic (see below). This is the only candidate that can lower area × delay.

Left: bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13 (grouped, same reason). All three are a counter multiplied by a constant power of two (counter*COL_ID_BITS, `counter_delay*`MULT_BITS, counter_delay*ROW_ID_BITS``), used only as the shift amount of a barrel shifter. Synthesis turns each into a wire relabelling — the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell and no DSP for them. Putting any of them on a block would add a DSP tile (they have no reset of their own, so they could not share the tile of the converted channels without extra reset logic), would add the block's 2-cycle latency into a purely combinational shift-amount path, and would save no soft logic at all. Area × delay can only get worse. Leave all three exactly as they are.

Why this needs a T2 (registers moved), and the one-cycle problem

In the baseline the product path is: operand FIFO memory (FF) → the FIFO's dpram output register (dout) → combinational multiply → the soft register mult → fifo_mult.din. That is two register stages from the FIFO memory to the value presented on fifo_mult.din.

mac_int_18x19 also has exactly two register stages (operand register, then the accumulator register). So if the block is fed from the existing vec_val/mat_val (which are already the FIFO's registered douts) the result lands one cycle late, which shifts every write into fifo_mult, hence mult_empty, hence the Accumulator, hence dout — not equivalent.

The fix is to let the block's own operand register be the FIFO output register for the two operand FIFOs, i.e. move that register into the block instead of adding one. The per-channel mat_val FIFO (fetcher) and vec_val FIFO (bvb) get a combinational (asynchronous) read port; their douts are then exactly one cycle earlier, and the block's a/b registers reproduce the removed registers bit-for-bit. Formally, with M(k) = mem[rp] evaluated on the state after edge k: the old registered dout during cycle c is M(c-2); the new combinational dout during cycle c is M(c-1); so the block's a_reg during cycle c+1 equals the old dout during cycle c+1. This is exact, including the read-during-write case, because both expressions are the same function of the same FIFO state, read one cycle apart. Only the multiply reads these two douts — mat_val_out in fetcher and val in bvb are driven straight from the FIFO douts and are read nowhere else in those modules; in spmv they go only to Big_Channel, in Big_Channel/Channel_Accumulator only to Channel, and in Channel only at line 3317. The col_id and row_id FIFOs in fetcher, the output FIFOs in Accumulator, and fifo_mult keep the ordinary registered-read FIFO and are not touched.

The block's output is a running sum, not a single product, so the product is recovered as the difference of consecutive accumulator values: mult_din = mac_out[15:0] - mac_out_d, where mac_out_d is mac_out[15:0] delayed one cycle. The difference is taken modulo 2^16, and an 8×8 product is < 2^16, so the recovered value is exact whatever the accumulator has wrapped to. mult_din is combinational and feeds fifo_mult.din, which is sampled by the FIFO memory on the write edge — the same cycle as the old mult register value was, so fifo_mult sees identical data at identical times. mult_wr_en and the two *_rd_en_reg registers are left exactly as they are, so the read enables going back to the operand FIFOs, mult_empty, done and everything downstream are cycle-identical.

Driving the block's reset (no assumptions needed)

reset must clear the accumulator before its first use (power-up X) and must never destroy a product that the design actually writes. A clear at edge k makes the recovered difference wrong during cycles k+1 and k+2 and correct from k+3. Drive, once in spmv and fanned out to all 32 blocks (one net, so the blocks may share tiles):

reg start_d1;                      // no reset on this register
always @(posedge clk) start_d1 <= start;
wire mac_rst = ~(start | start_d1);

fifo_mult is written during cycle c only if vec_val_rd_en_reg & mat_val_rd_en_reg held during cycle c-1, i.e. only if start was 1 during cycle c-2. If start is 1 during cycle c-2 then mac_rst is 0 during cycles c-2 and c-1, so the last clear is at or before edge c-3 and the difference during cycle c is already correct. This holds for start-up, for every mid-run rst pulse (including back-to-back pulses) and for restarts, with no assumption about the stimulus. It also guarantees at least one clear before the first write: rst forces all_empty <= 1 and start <= 0, so start is 0 for at least two consecutive cycles after any reset and the block is cleared then. start_d1 and mac_out_d must have no reset: at an edge that samples rst high the design still writes the product computed from pre-reset operands, and both registers must keep tracking through it. The rewrite relies on no initial value of either added register — any X they carry at power-up is gone by the third clock edge, well before start can rise.

Edits
  1. Add a module generic_fifo_sc_a_ar (copy of generic_fifo_sc_a, spmv.v:3586, same ports, same wp/rp/gb/empty/full logic driven by the same we/re/rst/clr) in which the dpram instance is replaced by
    ```
    reg [dw-1:0] mem [0:max_size-1];
    always @(posedge clk) if (we) mem[wp] <= din;
    assign dout = mem[rp];
    ```
    Do not change generic_fifo_sc_a itself.
  2. fetcher: change the 32 instances named fifo_mat_val_0 … fifo_mat_val_31 to generic_fifo_sc_a_ar. Leave fifo_col_id_* and fifo_row_id_* as generic_fifo_sc_a.
  3. bvb: change the 32 instances fifo_bvb_0 … fifo_bvb_31 to generic_fifo_sc_a_ar.
  4. spmv: add start_d1 and mac_rst as above; add .mac_rst(mac_rst) to the Big_Channel instance.
  5. Big_Channel: add input mac_rst; and pass .mac_rst(mac_rst) to all 32 Channel_Accumulator instances CH_0 … CH_31.
  6. Channel_Accumulator: add input mac_rst; and pass .mac_rst(mac_rst) to its Channel CH0.

  7. Channel: add input mac_rst;; delete reg [MULT_BITS-1:0] mult; and the mult <= vec_val * mat_val;` assignment; add

      wire [36:0] mac_out;
  mac_int_18x19 mac_u (.clk(clk), .reset(mac_rst),
                       .a({10'b0, vec_val}), .b({11'b0, mat_val}), .out(mac_out));
  reg [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mac_out_d;             // no reset
  always @(posedge clk) mac_out_d <= mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
  wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mult_din = mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - mac_out_d;

    change fifo_mult's .din(mult) to .din(mult_din), and keep the rest of the always block unchanged:

      always@(posedge clk) begin
      vec_val_rd_en_reg <= vec_val_rd_en;
      mat_val_rd_en_reg <= mat_val_rd_en;
      mult_wr_en <= (vec_val_rd_en_reg && mat_val_rd_en_reg);
  end

    (that last line is logically identical to the original if/else). Every input port of the block is connected; do not add a definition of mac_int_18x19 to spmv.v — it is supplied by the framework.

Blocks, tiles, stages
  • Blocks added: 32 (one per Channel). All 32 share clk and the single net mac_rst, so they pack two to a DSP tile: 16 DSP tiles, down from the baseline's 32, and far inside the 132 available. No other multiply moves onto a tile.
  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: one block; no block's output feeds another block's input. Its stage, input register to output register, is 2.219 ns, well under the slowest stage.
  • The slowest stage is untouched: it is the register → six LUTs → flip-flop path in fetcher (no multiply), 6.95433 ns at the baseline. The rewrite neither adds to it nor shortens it, so the period stays at about 6.954 ns and the gain is pure area. The new paths are all short: FIFO memory flip-flop (0.060) → 8:1 read mux (about 3 LUT levels) → routing → block input register (setup 0.019), and block output register (0.060) → 16-bit subtract → fifo_mult memory setup; both are a small fraction of 6.95 ns even with generous routing.
  • Soft logic that leaves a stage: the 32 soft/DSP 8×8 multipliers and the 32 mult registers leave the stage operand FIFO dout → multiply → mult, and the 64 operand-FIFO dout registers (8 bits each) leave the stage FIFO memory → dout register. Neither stage is the slowest. Soft logic added: per channel one 16-bit register (mac_out_d) and one 16-bit subtractor, plus one flip-flop in spmv (start_d1) for the whole design — a net of about +16 adder-BLEs and −16 flip-flops per channel, roughly +1 CLB per channel against 16 whole DSP tiles (≈145 CLBs) saved.
  • Register stages for the converted region, from operands to result: before — FIFO memory flip-flop, then the FIFO dout register, then the combinational multiply, then the mult register: 2 register stages from memory to the value on fifo_mult.din. After — FIFO memory flip-flop, combinational FIFO read, then the block's operand register, then the block's accumulator register, then the combinational subtract: again 2 register stages from memory to the value on fifo_mult.din. The latency of every signal leaving Channel is unchanged.

Risk worth knowing, stated rather than hedged around: the two operand FIFOs' 8-word memories become explicitly flip-flop based with a combinational read. If the baseline had them in block RAM, this trades 64 small memory blocks for soft logic; that is expected to be neutral-to-favourable in area but is the one quantity I could not read off the given numbers. Everything else above is exact.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if Channel were allowed to register the product one cycle later (e.g. the fifo_mult write pipelined by one cycle along with its empty flag), the block would drop straight in behind the existing registered FIFO douts and the two operand FIFOs would not need a combinational read port at all.

4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_18x19 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [17:0] a,
17	    input [18:0] b,

19	);

21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [17:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [18:0] b_reg;

25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
27	    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37

30	        if (reset) begin

32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end

1	//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
2	// Matrix vector multiplication design with sparsity
3	// This is a Sparse Matrix-Vector Multiplication accelerator design. 
4	// The mathematical formula is Y = M * X where M is input sparse matrix, 
5	// X is input dense vector and Y is output dense vector. We support 8-bit 
6	// precision. Vector X is loaded in on-chip banked memory before start of 
7	// the operation. The input to the accelerator are 3 values corresponding 
8	// to each matrix element: data, col, number of non zero values in a row. 
9	// There are 32 channels which can run in parallel. Each channel has a MAC 
10	// unit. Computation for each row of the matrix is assigned to a channel 
11	// dynamically based on the number of non zero values in that row. The final
12	// accumulated value is written in the output memory. The write address is 
13	// based on row number. Hence, write is out of order with respect to rows 
14	// in output vector.
15	// Multipliers in channels followed by accumulators, Banked Vector Buffer 
16	// (RAM and crossbar) to store vector elements. Arbiter to fetch data from 
17	// RAMs store matrix elements. Uses CISR encoding. Multiple FIFOs.
18	//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
19	
20	//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
21	// Author: Aatman Borda
22	//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
23	
24	//`define SIMULATION
25	`define COL_ID_BITS 8
26	`define ROW_ID_BITS 8
27	`define MAT_VAL_BITS 8
28	`define VEC_VAL_BITS 8
29	`define MULT_BITS (`VEC_VAL_BITS + `MAT_VAL_BITS)
30	`define NUM_CHANNEL 32
31	`define NUM_CHANNEL_BITS $clog2(`NUM_CHANNEL)
32	`define LANE_NUM (3 * `NUM_CHANNEL)
33	// `define LANE_NUM_BITS $clog2(`LANE_NUM)
34	//`define NUM_MAT_VALS 8864
35	`define NUM_MAT_VALS 4096
36	`define NUM_COL_IDS `NUM_MAT_VALS
37	`define NUM_ROW_IDS `NUM_MAT_VALS
38	`define NUM_VEC_VALS 128
39	
40	//`define MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH $clog2(`NUM_MAT_VALS)
41	`define MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH 12
42	`define COL_ID_ADDR_WIDTH `MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH
43	`define ROW_ID_ADDR_WIDTH `MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH
44	
45	`define FIFO_DEPTH 8
46	`define MAX_COLS (1<<`COL_ID_BITS)
47	
48	// MACROS for BVB
49	`define BYTES_PER_ADDR_PER_BRAM 1
50	`define NUM_BRAMS 1
51	`define VEC_VAL_BYTES (`VEC_VAL_BITS/8)
52	`define VEC_VAL_OFFSET $clog2(`VEC_VAL_BITS)
53	`define NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR_PER_BRAM (`BYTES_PER_ADDR_PER_BRAM/`VEC_VAL_BYTES)
54	`define NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR `NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR_PER_BRAM*`NUM_BRAMS
55	`define NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR_BITS $clog2(`NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR)
56	`define NUM_ADDR (`NUM_VEC_VALS/`NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR)+1

58	`define COUNTER_BITS $clog2(`NUM_ADDR)
59	`define LOCAL_ID_BITS `NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR_BITS

3507	
3508	Description
3509	===========
3510	
3511	I/Os
3512	----
3513	rst	low active, either sync. or async. master reset (see below how to select)
3514	clr	synchronous clear (just like reset but always synchronous), high active
3515	re	read enable, synchronous, high active
3516	we	read enable, synchronous, high active
3517	din	Data Input
3518	dout	Data Output
3519	
3520	full	Indicates the FIFO is full (combinatorial output)
3521	full_r	same as above, but registered output (see note below)
3522	empty	Indicates the FIFO is empty
3523	empty_r	same as above, but registered output (see note below)
3524	
3525	full_n		Indicates if the FIFO has space for N entries (combinatorial output)
3526	full_n_r	same as above, but registered output (see note below

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by reset. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of out (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is spmv.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: multiply.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products reach no sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: no sum.

idinstanceswhat it already is in the baseline
Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$7532on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, one to a tile on all 32 tiles (96 of the 128 slots open)
bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$571no DSP tile; a product by a constant factor that synthesis turns into shifts, so the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$111no DSP tile; a product by a constant factor that synthesis turns into shifts, so the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$131no DSP tile; a product by a constant factor that synthesis turns into shifts, so the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.

  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.

The plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 32
TILES: 16
STAGE_NS: 6.95433
MAPPING: per Channel instance (32 of them): clk -> block clk; mac_rst (new 1-bit input of Channel, driven from spmv as ~(start | start_d1)) -> block reset; {10'b0, vec_val} -> block a[18]; {11'b0, mat_val} -> block b[19]; block out[36:0] -> new wire mac_out, of which mac_out[15:0] is used; the value that used to be the register mult becomes the wire mult_din = mac_out[15:0] - mac_out_d, which drives fifo_mult.din. vec_val/mat_val are the (now combinational) douts of the per-channel mat_val FIFO in fetcher and vec_val FIFO in bvb.

PLAN:

Candidate regions

Converted: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (32 instances) — mult <= vec_val * mat_val (spmv.v:3317), the only real multiply in the design. In the baseline it sits one-to-a-tile on 32 DSP tiles in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode. 32 mac_int_18x19 blocks all driven by one clock and one shared reset net pack two to a tile, so the design goes from 32 DSP tiles to 16 — a saving of 16 × 253779 = 4.06e6 MWTA (~7% of the 5.856e7 baseline) against a small amount of added soft logic (see below). This is the only candidate that can lower area × delay.

Left: bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13 (grouped, same reason). All three are a counter multiplied by a constant power of two (counter*COL_ID_BITS, `counter_delay*`MULT_BITS, counter_delay*ROW_ID_BITS``), used only as the shift amount of a barrel shifter. Synthesis turns each into a wire relabelling — the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell and no DSP for them. Putting any of them on a block would add a DSP tile (they have no reset of their own, so they could not share the tile of the converted channels without extra reset logic), would add the block's 2-cycle latency into a purely combinational shift-amount path, and would save no soft logic at all. Area × delay can only get worse. Leave all three exactly as they are.

Why this needs a T2 (registers moved), and the one-cycle problem

In the baseline the product path is: operand FIFO memory (FF) → the FIFO's dpram output register (dout) → combinational multiply → the soft register mult → fifo_mult.din. That is two register stages from the FIFO memory to the value presented on fifo_mult.din.

mac_int_18x19 also has exactly two register stages (operand register, then the accumulator register). So if the block is fed from the existing vec_val/mat_val (which are already the FIFO's registered douts) the result lands one cycle late, which shifts every write into fifo_mult, hence mult_empty, hence the Accumulator, hence dout — not equivalent.

The fix is to let the block's own operand register be the FIFO output register for the two operand FIFOs, i.e. move that register into the block instead of adding one. The per-channel mat_val FIFO (fetcher) and vec_val FIFO (bvb) get a combinational (asynchronous) read port; their douts are then exactly one cycle earlier, and the block's a/b registers reproduce the removed registers bit-for-bit. Formally, with M(k) = mem[rp] evaluated on the state after edge k: the old registered dout during cycle c is M(c-2); the new combinational dout during cycle c is M(c-1); so the block's a_reg during cycle c+1 equals the old dout during cycle c+1. This is exact, including the read-during-write case, because both expressions are the same function of the same FIFO state, read one cycle apart. Only the multiply reads these two douts — mat_val_out in fetcher and val in bvb are driven straight from the FIFO douts and are read nowhere else in those modules; in spmv they go only to Big_Channel, in Big_Channel/Channel_Accumulator only to Channel, and in Channel only at line 3317. The col_id and row_id FIFOs in fetcher, the output FIFOs in Accumulator, and fifo_mult keep the ordinary registered-read FIFO and are not touched.

The block's output is a running sum, not a single product, so the product is recovered as the difference of consecutive accumulator values: mult_din = mac_out[15:0] - mac_out_d, where mac_out_d is mac_out[15:0] delayed one cycle. The difference is taken modulo 2^16, and an 8×8 product is < 2^16, so the recovered value is exact whatever the accumulator has wrapped to. mult_din is combinational and feeds fifo_mult.din, which is sampled by the FIFO memory on the write edge — the same cycle as the old mult register value was, so fifo_mult sees identical data at identical times. mult_wr_en and the two *_rd_en_reg registers are left exactly as they are, so the read enables going back to the operand FIFOs, mult_empty, done and everything downstream are cycle-identical.

Driving the block's reset (no assumptions needed)

reset must clear the accumulator before its first use (power-up X) and must never destroy a product that the design actually writes. A clear at edge k makes the recovered difference wrong during cycles k+1 and k+2 and correct from k+3. Drive, once in spmv and fanned out to all 32 blocks (one net, so the blocks may share tiles):

reg start_d1;                      // no reset on this register
always @(posedge clk) start_d1 <= start;
wire mac_rst = ~(start | start_d1);

fifo_mult is written during cycle c only if vec_val_rd_en_reg & mat_val_rd_en_reg held during cycle c-1, i.e. only if start was 1 during cycle c-2. If start is 1 during cycle c-2 then mac_rst is 0 during cycles c-2 and c-1, so the last clear is at or before edge c-3 and the difference during cycle c is already correct. This holds for start-up, for every mid-run rst pulse (including back-to-back pulses) and for restarts, with no assumption about the stimulus. It also guarantees at least one clear before the first write: rst forces all_empty <= 1 and start <= 0, so start is 0 for at least two consecutive cycles after any reset and the block is cleared then. start_d1 and mac_out_d must have no reset: at an edge that samples rst high the design still writes the product computed from pre-reset operands, and both registers must keep tracking through it. The rewrite relies on no initial value of either added register — any X they carry at power-up is gone by the third clock edge, well before start can rise.

Edits
  1. Add a module generic_fifo_sc_a_ar (copy of generic_fifo_sc_a, spmv.v:3586, same ports, same wp/rp/gb/empty/full logic driven by the same we/re/rst/clr) in which the dpram instance is replaced by
    ```
    reg [dw-1:0] mem [0:max_size-1];
    always @(posedge clk) if (we) mem[wp] <= din;
    assign dout = mem[rp];
    ```
    Do not change generic_fifo_sc_a itself.
  2. fetcher: change the 32 instances named fifo_mat_val_0 … fifo_mat_val_31 to generic_fifo_sc_a_ar. Leave fifo_col_id_* and fifo_row_id_* as generic_fifo_sc_a.
  3. bvb: change the 32 instances fifo_bvb_0 … fifo_bvb_31 to generic_fifo_sc_a_ar.
  4. spmv: add start_d1 and mac_rst as above; add .mac_rst(mac_rst) to the Big_Channel instance.
  5. Big_Channel: add input mac_rst; and pass .mac_rst(mac_rst) to all 32 Channel_Accumulator instances CH_0 … CH_31.
  6. Channel_Accumulator: add input mac_rst; and pass .mac_rst(mac_rst) to its Channel CH0.

  7. Channel: add input mac_rst;; delete reg [MULT_BITS-1:0] mult; and the mult <= vec_val * mat_val;` assignment; add

      wire [36:0] mac_out;
  mac_int_18x19 mac_u (.clk(clk), .reset(mac_rst),
                       .a({10'b0, vec_val}), .b({11'b0, mat_val}), .out(mac_out));
  reg [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mac_out_d;             // no reset
  always @(posedge clk) mac_out_d <= mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
  wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mult_din = mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - mac_out_d;

    change fifo_mult's .din(mult) to .din(mult_din), and keep the rest of the always block unchanged:

      always@(posedge clk) begin
      vec_val_rd_en_reg <= vec_val_rd_en;
      mat_val_rd_en_reg <= mat_val_rd_en;
      mult_wr_en <= (vec_val_rd_en_reg && mat_val_rd_en_reg);
  end

    (that last line is logically identical to the original if/else). Every input port of the block is connected; do not add a definition of mac_int_18x19 to spmv.v — it is supplied by the framework.

Blocks, tiles, stages
  • Blocks added: 32 (one per Channel). All 32 share clk and the single net mac_rst, so they pack two to a DSP tile: 16 DSP tiles, down from the baseline's 32, and far inside the 132 available. No other multiply moves onto a tile.
  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: one block; no block's output feeds another block's input. Its stage, input register to output register, is 2.219 ns, well under the slowest stage.
  • The slowest stage is untouched: it is the register → six LUTs → flip-flop path in fetcher (no multiply), 6.95433 ns at the baseline. The rewrite neither adds to it nor shortens it, so the period stays at about 6.954 ns and the gain is pure area. The new paths are all short: FIFO memory flip-flop (0.060) → 8:1 read mux (about 3 LUT levels) → routing → block input register (setup 0.019), and block output register (0.060) → 16-bit subtract → fifo_mult memory setup; both are a small fraction of 6.95 ns even with generous routing.
  • Soft logic that leaves a stage: the 32 soft/DSP 8×8 multipliers and the 32 mult registers leave the stage operand FIFO dout → multiply → mult, and the 64 operand-FIFO dout registers (8 bits each) leave the stage FIFO memory → dout register. Neither stage is the slowest. Soft logic added: per channel one 16-bit register (mac_out_d) and one 16-bit subtractor, plus one flip-flop in spmv (start_d1) for the whole design — a net of about +16 adder-BLEs and −16 flip-flops per channel, roughly +1 CLB per channel against 16 whole DSP tiles (≈145 CLBs) saved.
  • Register stages for the converted region, from operands to result: before — FIFO memory flip-flop, then the FIFO dout register, then the combinational multiply, then the mult register: 2 register stages from memory to the value on fifo_mult.din. After — FIFO memory flip-flop, combinational FIFO read, then the block's operand register, then the block's accumulator register, then the combinational subtract: again 2 register stages from memory to the value on fifo_mult.din. The latency of every signal leaving Channel is unchanged.

Risk worth knowing, stated rather than hedged around: the two operand FIFOs' 8-word memories become explicitly flip-flop based with a combinational read. If the baseline had them in block RAM, this trades 64 small memory blocks for soft logic; that is expected to be neutral-to-favourable in area but is the one quantity I could not read off the given numbers. Everything else above is exact.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if Channel were allowed to register the product one cycle later (e.g. the fifo_mult write pipelined by one cycle along with its empty flag), the block would drop straight in behind the existing registered FIFO douts and the two operand FIFOs would not need a combinational read port at all.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_spmv.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

response

I'll read the design source before judging anything.

APPROVE

Verified against spmv.v:

  • Candidate coverage — all four regions addressed: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 converted; the three constant-factor multiplies (spmv.v:163, spmv.v:172, spmv.v:2490) left, grouped with one reason. All three are indeed counter/counter_delay times a power-of-two macro used only as a shift amount (data_out >> (counter_delay*16), addr_out >> (counter_delay*8), id >> (counter*8)), and spmv.v:172 is the live branch since QUARTUS is undefined.
  • Visibility — mat_val_out occurs 34 times (port decl spmv.v:307, the spmv connection spmv.v:229, 32 .dout(...) slices) and val[...] 32 times (only .dout(...)); in spmv they go to Big_Channel only (spmv.v:279, 282), which merely slices them to Channel_Accumulator/Channel, where the sole reader is spmv.v:3317. The one-cycle-earlier dout is therefore invisible elsewhere; empty/full/wp/rp/gb are untouched, so every handshake is unchanged.
  • Register move is exact — dpram has wren_a tied to 1'b0 (spmv.v:3624), so out_a <= ram[rp] fires unconditionally each edge and, by nonblocking semantics, reads memory state after the same-edge port-b write. Old dout during cycle k+1 = R_k[rp_k]; new combinational dout during cycle k = R_k[rp_k], which the block's a_reg holds during cycle k+1. Identical, read-during-write included.
  • Timing of the recovered product — original mult during cycle t = R_{t-2}[rp_{t-2}] products (spmv.v:3312-3319); mac_out[15:0]-mac_out_d during cycle t = product of block inputs during t-2 = the same R_{t-2}[rp_{t-2}] pair. mult_wr_en (unchanged logic, the if/else rewrite is equivalent) and the two *_rd_en_reg keep fifo_mult writes at identical cycles with identical data. 16-bit difference is exact since an 8×8 product < 2^16.
  • Reset — mac_rst = ~(start|start_d1) with no reset on start_d1: a write during cycle c requires start=1 during c-2 (spmv.v:3309-3310, 3313-3318), which forces mac_rst=0 over c-2 and c-1, so no clear can land on the two edges that would corrupt that product — including the edge where rst (spmv.v:144-147) drops start while the pre-reset product is still in flight. rst guarantees start=0 for ≥2 cycles, so a clear always precedes the first write; no reliance on the power-up value of start_d1 or mac_out_d (an X on either is gone before start can rise, and nothing is written meanwhile). Mid-run rst also resets fifo_mult's pointers, so any stale word written at that edge is overwritten before it can be read. No ASSUMES needed, and none claimed.
  • Required statements — blocks added (32), tiles after (16, one shared clk+mac_rst net so two per tile, within 132); longest block series per stage (1, 2.219 ns) against the slowest stage (6.95433 ns); soft logic leaving each stage (multipliers + mult registers from the dout→multiply→mult stage, the 64 dout registers from the memory→dout stage) with the statement that neither is the slowest; register stages before and after (2 and 2, latency out of Channel unchanged).

The minor off-by-one in the prose ("correct from k+3", "clear at or before edge c-3" — the true bounds are k+2 and c-2) is conservative in the safe direction and does not change the conclusion.

2	// Matrix vector multiplication design with sparsity
3	// This is a Sparse Matrix-Vector Multiplication accelerator design. 
4	// The mathematical formula is Y = M * X where M is input sparse matrix, 
5	// X is input dense vector and Y is output dense vector. We support 8-bit 
6	// precision. Vector X is loaded in on-chip banked memory before start of 
7	// the operation. The input to the accelerator are 3 values corresponding 
8	// to each matrix element: data, col, number of non zero values in a row.

Read completed /work/sim_reference/mac_int_18x19.v

 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/mac_int_18x19.v"

output
1	// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_18x19 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [17:0] a,
17	    input [18:0] b,
18	    output reg [36:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [17:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [18:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
27	    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule
41

Task

Edit spmv.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by reset. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of out (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Region in the approved plan: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (module Channel, operation multiply, datatype int8). The framework found no sum that its products feed.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work

  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.

The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 32
TILES: 16
STAGE_NS: 6.95433
MAPPING: per Channel instance (32 of them): clk -> block clk; mac_rst (new 1-bit input of Channel, driven from spmv as ~(start | start_d1)) -> block reset; {10'b0, vec_val} -> block a[18]; {11'b0, mat_val} -> block b[19]; block out[36:0] -> new wire mac_out, of which mac_out[15:0] is used; the value that used to be the register mult becomes the wire mult_din = mac_out[15:0] - mac_out_d, which drives fifo_mult.din. vec_val/mat_val are the (now combinational) douts of the per-channel mat_val FIFO in fetcher and vec_val FIFO in bvb.

PLAN:

Candidate regions

Converted: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (32 instances) — mult <= vec_val * mat_val (spmv.v:3317), the only real multiply in the design. In the baseline it sits one-to-a-tile on 32 DSP tiles in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode. 32 mac_int_18x19 blocks all driven by one clock and one shared reset net pack two to a tile, so the design goes from 32 DSP tiles to 16 — a saving of 16 × 253779 = 4.06e6 MWTA (~7% of the 5.856e7 baseline) against a small amount of added soft logic (see below). This is the only candidate that can lower area × delay.

Left: bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13 (grouped, same reason). All three are a counter multiplied by a constant power of two (counter*COL_ID_BITS, `counter_delay*`MULT_BITS, counter_delay*ROW_ID_BITS``), used only as the shift amount of a barrel shifter. Synthesis turns each into a wire relabelling — the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell and no DSP for them. Putting any of them on a block would add a DSP tile (they have no reset of their own, so they could not share the tile of the converted channels without extra reset logic), would add the block's 2-cycle latency into a purely combinational shift-amount path, and would save no soft logic at all. Area × delay can only get worse. Leave all three exactly as they are.

Why this needs a T2 (registers moved), and the one-cycle problem

In the baseline the product path is: operand FIFO memory (FF) → the FIFO's dpram output register (dout) → combinational multiply → the soft register mult → fifo_mult.din. That is two register stages from the FIFO memory to the value presented on fifo_mult.din.

mac_int_18x19 also has exactly two register stages (operand register, then the accumulator register). So if the block is fed from the existing vec_val/mat_val (which are already the FIFO's registered douts) the result lands one cycle late, which shifts every write into fifo_mult, hence mult_empty, hence the Accumulator, hence dout — not equivalent.

The fix is to let the block's own operand register be the FIFO output register for the two operand FIFOs, i.e. move that register into the block instead of adding one. The per-channel mat_val FIFO (fetcher) and vec_val FIFO (bvb) get a combinational (asynchronous) read port; their douts are then exactly one cycle earlier, and the block's a/b registers reproduce the removed registers bit-for-bit. Formally, with M(k) = mem[rp] evaluated on the state after edge k: the old registered dout during cycle c is M(c-2); the new combinational dout during cycle c is M(c-1); so the block's a_reg during cycle c+1 equals the old dout during cycle c+1. This is exact, including the read-during-write case, because both expressions are the same function of the same FIFO state, read one cycle apart. Only the multiply reads these two douts — mat_val_out in fetcher and val in bvb are driven straight from the FIFO douts and are read nowhere else in those modules; in spmv they go only to Big_Channel, in Big_Channel/Channel_Accumulator only to Channel, and in Channel only at line 3317. The col_id and row_id FIFOs in fetcher, the output FIFOs in Accumulator, and fifo_mult keep the ordinary registered-read FIFO and are not touched.

The block's output is a running sum, not a single product, so the product is recovered as the difference of consecutive accumulator values: mult_din = mac_out[15:0] - mac_out_d, where mac_out_d is mac_out[15:0] delayed one cycle. The difference is taken modulo 2^16, and an 8×8 product is < 2^16, so the recovered value is exact whatever the accumulator has wrapped to. mult_din is combinational and feeds fifo_mult.din, which is sampled by the FIFO memory on the write edge — the same cycle as the old mult register value was, so fifo_mult sees identical data at identical times. mult_wr_en and the two *_rd_en_reg registers are left exactly as they are, so the read enables going back to the operand FIFOs, mult_empty, done and everything downstream are cycle-identical.

Driving the block's reset (no assumptions needed)

reset must clear the accumulator before its first use (power-up X) and must never destroy a product that the design actually writes. A clear at edge k makes the recovered difference wrong during cycles k+1 and k+2 and correct from k+3. Drive, once in spmv and fanned out to all 32 blocks (one net, so the blocks may share tiles):

reg start_d1;                      // no reset on this register
always @(posedge clk) start_d1 <= start;
wire mac_rst = ~(start | start_d1);

fifo_mult is written during cycle c only if vec_val_rd_en_reg & mat_val_rd_en_reg held during cycle c-1, i.e. only if start was 1 during cycle c-2. If start is 1 during cycle c-2 then mac_rst is 0 during cycles c-2 and c-1, so the last clear is at or before edge c-3 and the difference during cycle c is already correct. This holds for start-up, for every mid-run rst pulse (including back-to-back pulses) and for restarts, with no assumption about the stimulus. It also guarantees at least one clear before the first write: rst forces all_empty <= 1 and start <= 0, so start is 0 for at least two consecutive cycles after any reset and the block is cleared then. start_d1 and mac_out_d must have no reset: at an edge that samples rst high the design still writes the product computed from pre-reset operands, and both registers must keep tracking through it. The rewrite relies on no initial value of either added register — any X they carry at power-up is gone by the third clock edge, well before start can rise.

Edits
  1. Add a module generic_fifo_sc_a_ar (copy of generic_fifo_sc_a, spmv.v:3586, same ports, same wp/rp/gb/empty/full logic driven by the same we/re/rst/clr) in which the dpram instance is replaced by
    ```
    reg [dw-1:0] mem [0:max_size-1];
    always @(posedge clk) if (we) mem[wp] <= din;
    assign dout = mem[rp];
    ```
    Do not change generic_fifo_sc_a itself.
  2. fetcher: change the 32 instances named fifo_mat_val_0 … fifo_mat_val_31 to generic_fifo_sc_a_ar. Leave fifo_col_id_* and fifo_row_id_* as generic_fifo_sc_a.
  3. bvb: change the 32 instances fifo_bvb_0 … fifo_bvb_31 to generic_fifo_sc_a_ar.
  4. spmv: add start_d1 and mac_rst as above; add .mac_rst(mac_rst) to the Big_Channel instance.
  5. Big_Channel: add input mac_rst; and pass .mac_rst(mac_rst) to all 32 Channel_Accumulator instances CH_0 … CH_31.
  6. Channel_Accumulator: add input mac_rst; and pass .mac_rst(mac_rst) to its Channel CH0.

  7. Channel: add input mac_rst;; delete reg [MULT_BITS-1:0] mult; and the mult <= vec_val * mat_val;` assignment; add

      wire [36:0] mac_out;
  mac_int_18x19 mac_u (.clk(clk), .reset(mac_rst),
                       .a({10'b0, vec_val}), .b({11'b0, mat_val}), .out(mac_out));
  reg [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mac_out_d;             // no reset
  always @(posedge clk) mac_out_d <= mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
  wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mult_din = mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - mac_out_d;

    change fifo_mult's .din(mult) to .din(mult_din), and keep the rest of the always block unchanged:

      always@(posedge clk) begin
      vec_val_rd_en_reg <= vec_val_rd_en;
      mat_val_rd_en_reg <= mat_val_rd_en;
      mult_wr_en <= (vec_val_rd_en_reg && mat_val_rd_en_reg);
  end

    (that last line is logically identical to the original if/else). Every input port of the block is connected; do not add a definition of mac_int_18x19 to spmv.v — it is supplied by the framework.

Blocks, tiles, stages
  • Blocks added: 32 (one per Channel). All 32 share clk and the single net mac_rst, so they pack two to a DSP tile: 16 DSP tiles, down from the baseline's 32, and far inside the 132 available. No other multiply moves onto a tile.
  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: one block; no block's output feeds another block's input. Its stage, input register to output register, is 2.219 ns, well under the slowest stage.
  • The slowest stage is untouched: it is the register → six LUTs → flip-flop path in fetcher (no multiply), 6.95433 ns at the baseline. The rewrite neither adds to it nor shortens it, so the period stays at about 6.954 ns and the gain is pure area. The new paths are all short: FIFO memory flip-flop (0.060) → 8:1 read mux (about 3 LUT levels) → routing → block input register (setup 0.019), and block output register (0.060) → 16-bit subtract → fifo_mult memory setup; both are a small fraction of 6.95 ns even with generous routing.
  • Soft logic that leaves a stage: the 32 soft/DSP 8×8 multipliers and the 32 mult registers leave the stage operand FIFO dout → multiply → mult, and the 64 operand-FIFO dout registers (8 bits each) leave the stage FIFO memory → dout register. Neither stage is the slowest. Soft logic added: per channel one 16-bit register (mac_out_d) and one 16-bit subtractor, plus one flip-flop in spmv (start_d1) for the whole design — a net of about +16 adder-BLEs and −16 flip-flops per channel, roughly +1 CLB per channel against 16 whole DSP tiles (≈145 CLBs) saved.
  • Register stages for the converted region, from operands to result: before — FIFO memory flip-flop, then the FIFO dout register, then the combinational multiply, then the mult register: 2 register stages from memory to the value on fifo_mult.din. After — FIFO memory flip-flop, combinational FIFO read, then the block's operand register, then the block's accumulator register, then the combinational subtract: again 2 register stages from memory to the value on fifo_mult.din. The latency of every signal leaving Channel is unchanged.

Risk worth knowing, stated rather than hedged around: the two operand FIFOs' 8-word memories become explicitly flip-flop based with a combinational read. If the baseline had them in block RAM, this trades 64 small memory blocks for soft logic; that is expected to be neutral-to-favourable in area but is the one quantity I could not read off the given numbers. Everything else above is exact.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_spmv.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"

// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
// output width; the product alone can fill it).
// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
module mac_int_18x19 (
    input clk,
    input reset,
    input [17:0] a,
    input [18:0] b,
    output reg [36:0] out
);

    // stage 1: the input registers
    reg [17:0] a_reg;
    reg [18:0] b_reg;

    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (reset) begin
            out <= 0;
            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin
            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
        end
    end

endmodule

//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
// Matrix vector multiplication design with sparsity
// This is a Sparse Matrix-Vector Multiplication accelerator design. 
// The mathematical formula is Y = M * X where M is input sparse matrix, 
// X is input dense vector and Y is output dense vector. We support 8-bit 
// precision. Vector X is loaded in on-chip banked memory before start of 
// the operation. The input to the accelerator are 3 values corresponding 
// to each matrix element: data, col, number of non zero values in a row. 
// There are 32 channels which can run in parallel. Each channel has a MAC 
// unit. Computation for each row of the matrix is assigned to a channel 
// dynamically based on the number of non zero values in that row. The final
// accumulated value is written in the output memory. The write address is 
// based on row number. Hence, write is out of order with respect to rows 
// in output vector.
// Multipliers in channels followed by accumulators, Banked Vector Buffer 
// (RAM and crossbar) to store vector elements. Arbiter to fetch data from 
// RAMs store matrix elements. Uses CISR encoding. Multiple FIFOs.
//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
// Author: Aatman Borda
//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

//`define SIMULATION
`define COL_ID_BITS 8
`define ROW_ID_BITS 8
`define MAT_VAL_BITS 8
`define VEC_VAL_BITS 8
`define MULT_BITS (`VEC_VAL_BITS + `MAT_VAL_BITS)
`define NUM_CHANNEL 32
`define NUM_CHANNEL_BITS $clog2(`NUM_CHANNEL)
`define LANE_NUM (3 * `NUM_CHANNEL)
// `define LANE_NUM_BITS $clog2(`LANE_NUM)
//`define NUM_MAT_VALS 8864
`define NUM_MAT_VALS 4096
`define NUM_COL_IDS `NUM_MAT_VALS
`define NUM_ROW_IDS `NUM_MAT_VALS
`define NUM_VEC_VALS 128

//`define MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH $clog2(`NUM_MAT_VALS)
`define MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH 12
`define COL_ID_ADDR_WIDTH `MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH
`define ROW_ID_ADDR_WIDTH `MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH

`define FIFO_DEPTH 8
`define MAX_COLS (1<<`COL_ID_BITS)

// MACROS for BVB
`define BYTES_PER_ADDR_PER_BRAM 1
`define NUM_BRAMS 1
`define VEC_VAL_BYTES (`VEC_VAL_BITS/8)
`define VEC_VAL_OFFSET $clog2(`VEC_VAL_BITS)
`define NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR_PER_BRAM (`BYTES_PER_ADDR_PER_BRAM/`VEC_VAL_BYTES)
`define NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR `NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR_PER_BRAM*`NUM_BRAMS
`define NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR_BITS $clog2(`NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR)
`define NUM_ADDR (`NUM_VEC_VALS/`NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR)+1
`define BVB_AWIDTH `COL_ID_BITS
`define COUNTER_BITS $clog2(`NUM_ADDR)
`define LOCAL_ID_BITS `NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR_BITS

module spmv(

  input [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] row_id_din,

  input col_id_wren,
  input row_id_wren,

  input [`MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH-1:0] mat_val_addr_ext,
  input [`COL_ID_ADDR_WIDTH-1:0] col_id_addr_ext,
  input [`ROW_ID_ADDR_WIDTH-1:0] row_id_addr_ext,

	input [`NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR*`VEC_VAL_BITS-1:0] vector_din,

	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] mat_val_rd_en;

	// The above the FIFOs are called fetcher FIFOs as they are a part of fetcher module and are filled by fetcher.

	// Fetcher module signals. There are just the above three put together.
	wire [((`ROW_ID_BITS+`COL_ID_BITS+`MAT_VAL_BITS)*`NUM_CHANNEL)-1:0] fetcher_out;

module Channel(
	input clk, 
	input rst,
	input start,
	input fetcher_done,

	input [`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0] mat_val,
	input mat_val_empty,
	output mat_val_rd_en,

	input [`VEC_VAL_BITS-1:0] vec_val,
	input vec_val_empty,
	output vec_val_rd_en,

	output [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mult_out,
	output mult_empty,
	input mult_rd_en,

	output done 
	);

	parameter FIFO_DEPTH = 8;
	// parameter FIFO_DEPTH_BITS = `LOG2(FIFO_DEPTH);
	parameter FIFO_DEPTH_BITS = $clog2(8);

	reg [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mult;
	reg mult_wr_en;
	wire mult_full;
	
	reg vec_val_rd_en_reg;
	reg mat_val_rd_en_reg;

	generic_fifo_sc_a #(
		.dw(`MULT_BITS),
		.aw(FIFO_DEPTH_BITS)
		) fifo_mult (
		.clk(clk),
		.rst(rst),
		.clr(1'b0),
		.din(mult),
		.we(mult_wr_en),
		.re(mult_rd_en),
		.dout(mult_out),
		.full(mult_full),
		.empty(mult_empty)
	);

	assign done = mat_val_empty & fetcher_done & start;

	assign vec_val_rd_en = start & (~vec_val_empty) & (~mat_val_empty) & (~mult_full);
	assign mat_val_rd_en = start & (~vec_val_empty) & (~mat_val_empty) & (~mult_full);

	always@(posedge clk) begin
		vec_val_rd_en_reg <= vec_val_rd_en;
		mat_val_rd_en_reg <= mat_val_rd_en;

		if(vec_val_rd_en_reg && mat_val_rd_en_reg) begin
			mult <= vec_val * mat_val;
			mult_wr_en <= 1;
		end
		else begin
			mult_wr_en <= 0;
		end
	end
endmodule

module Channel_Accumulator(

	input rst, 
	input start,
	input fetcher_done,

	input mat_val_empty,

	input row_id_empty,
	output row_id_rd_en,

	output [`MULT_BITS-1:0] data_out,
	output data_out_empty,
	output [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] addr_out,
	input out_rd_en,

	output done
	);

	wire mult_empty;
	wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mult_out;
	wire mult_done;

				.start(start),
				.fetcher_done(fetcher_done),
				.mat_val(mat_val),

					.data_out_empty(data_out_empty),
					.addr_out(addr_out),
					.out_rd_en(out_rd_en),
					.done(done)			
	);

// synchronous reset, the second for asynchronous reset

// `define SC_FIFO_ASYNC_RESET				// Uncomment for Syncr. reset
// `define SC_FIFO_ASYNC_RESET	or posedge rst		// Uncomment for Async. reset


module generic_fifo_sc_a
	#(parameter dw=8,
	  parameter aw=8)
	(clk, rst, clr, din, we, dout, re,
	full, empty);

localparam max_size = 1<<aw;

input 			clk, rst, clr;
input 	[dw-1:0]	din;
input 			we;
output	[dw-1:0]	dout;
input 	wire		re;
output			full;
output			empty;

////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
//
// Local Wires
//
wire 	[dw-1:0]	din_nc;
wire 	[dw-1:0]	out_nc;
reg		[aw-1:0]	wp;
wire	[aw-1:0]	wp_pl1;
reg		[aw-1:0]	rp;
wire	[aw-1:0]	rp_pl1;
reg					gb;

////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
//
// Memory Block
//
dpram #(
	.AWIDTH(aw),
	.DWIDTH(dw)
	) u0 (
	.clk(clk),
	.address_a(rp),
	.wren_a(1'b0),
	.data_a(din_nc),
	.out_a(dout),
	.address_b(wp),
	.wren_b(we),
	.data_b(din),
	.out_b(out_nc)
);


////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
//
// Misc Logic
//

always @(posedge clk)
	if(rst)	wp <= {aw{1'b0}};
	else
	if(clr)		wp <= {aw{1'b0}};
	else
	if(we)		wp <= wp_pl1;

assign wp_pl1 = wp + 1'b1;

always @(posedge clk)
	if(rst)		rp <= {aw{1'b0}};
	else
	if(clr)		rp <= {aw{1'b0}};
	else
	if(re)		rp <= rp_pl1;

assign rp_pl1 = rp + 1'b1;

////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
//
// Combinatorial Full & Empty Flags
//

assign empty = ((wp == rp) & !gb);
assign full  = ((wp == rp) &  gb);

// Guard Bit ...
always @(posedge clk)
	if(rst)						gb <= 1'b0;
	else
	if(clr)						gb <= 1'b0;
	else
	if((wp_pl1 == rp) & we)		gb <= 1'b1;
	else
	if(re)						gb <= 1'b0;

endmodule

module dpram #(
	parameter DWIDTH = 32,
	parameter AWIDTH = 10
  )
  (
	clk,
	address_a,
	address_b,
	wren_a,
	wren_b,
	data_a,
	data_b,
	out_a,
	out_b
);

localparam NUM_WORDS=1<<AWIDTH;

input clk;
input [(AWIDTH-1):0] address_a;
input [(AWIDTH-1):0] address_b;
input  wren_a;
input  wren_b;
input [(DWIDTH-1):0] data_a;
input [(DWIDTH-1):0] data_b;
output reg [(DWIDTH-1):0] out_a;
output reg [(DWIDTH-1):0] out_b;

`ifndef hard_mem

	reg [DWIDTH-1:0] ram[NUM_WORDS-1:0];

	always @ (posedge clk) begin
		if (wren_a) begin
			ram[address_a] <= data_a;
		end
		else begin
			out_a <= ram[address_a];
		end
	end
	  
	always @ (posedge clk) begin 
		if (wren_b) begin
			ram[address_b] <= data_b;
		end 
		else begin
			out_b <= ram[address_b];
		end
	end

`else
defparam u_dual_port_ram.ADDR_WIDTH = AWIDTH;
defparam u_dual_port_ram.DATA_WIDTH = DWIDTH;

	dual_port_ram u_dual_port_ram(
	.addr1(address_a),
	.we1(wren_a),
	.data1(data_a),
	.out1(out_a),
	.addr2(address_b),
	.we2(wren_b),
	.data2(data_b),
	.out2(out_b),
	.clk(clk)
	);

`endif

endmodule

module bvb(
	input clk,
	input rst,
	input start,
	// input from column ID FIFO
	input  [(`NUM_CHANNEL*`COL_ID_BITS)-1:0] id,
	input  [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] id_empty,
	// input to the column ID FIFO
	output [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] id_rd_en,

	// vector value FIFO outputs
	output [(`NUM_CHANNEL*`VEC_VAL_BITS)-1:0] val,
	output [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0]   val_empty,
	// input to vector value FIFO
	input  [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0]   val_rd_en,
	input [`NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR*`VEC_VAL_BITS-1:0] vector_din,
  input vector_wren,
  input [`BVB_AWIDTH-1:0] vector_addr_ext
);

	parameter FIFO_DEPTH_BITS = $clog2(`FIFO_DEPTH);

	reg [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] val_wr_en;
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] val_full;

	wire [`NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR*`VEC_VAL_BITS-1:0] ram_out;

	wire fifo_in_val;

	wire [`NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR*`VEC_VAL_BITS-1:0] din_nc;

	reg [`COL_ID_BITS-1:0] rd_addr;

	wire id_empty_shifted;
	wire val_full_shifted;

	wire id_rd_en_local;
	reg id_rd_en_local_reg;

	reg [`NUM_CHANNEL_BITS-1:0] counter;
	reg [`NUM_CHANNEL_BITS-1:0] counter_delay;

  wire [`BVB_AWIDTH-1:0] vector_addr;
  assign vector_addr = vector_wren ? vector_addr_ext : rd_addr;

	spram #(
	`ifdef SIMULATION
		.INIT("/home/aatman/Desktop/SpMV/src/coe/vec_val.txt"),
	`endif
		.AWIDTH(`BVB_AWIDTH),
		.NUM_WORDS(`NUM_ADDR),
		.DWIDTH(`NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR*`VEC_VAL_BITS)
		) vector_ram (
			.clk(clk), 
			.wren(vector_wren),
			.address(vector_addr), 
			.din(vector_din), 
			.dout(ram_out)
		);

	generic_fifo_sc_a #( 
		.dw(`VEC_VAL_BITS), 
		.aw(FIFO_DEPTH_BITS) 
	) fifo_bvb_0 ( 
		.clk(clk), // input clk 
		.rst(rst), 
		.clr(1'b0), 
		.din(ram_out), 
		.we(val_wr_en[0]), // input wr_en 
		.re(val_rd_en[0]), // input rd_en 
		.dout(val[(0+1)*`VEC_VAL_BITS-1:0*`VEC_VAL_BITS]), 
		.full(val_full[0]), // output full 
		.empty(val_empty[0]) // output empty 
	);

	generic_fifo_sc_a #(
Bash completed sed -n '2440,2495p' spmv.v
input
{
 "command": "sed -n '2440,2495p' spmv.v"
}
output
		.dout(val[(29+1)*`VEC_VAL_BITS-1:29*`VEC_VAL_BITS]), 
		.full(val_full[29]), // output full 
		.empty(val_empty[29]) // output empty 
	);

	generic_fifo_sc_a #( 
		.dw(`VEC_VAL_BITS), 
		.aw(FIFO_DEPTH_BITS) 
	) fifo_bvb_30 ( 
		.clk(clk), // input clk 
		.rst(rst), 
		.clr(1'b0), 
		.din(ram_out), 
		.we(val_wr_en[30]), // input wr_en 
		.re(val_rd_en[30]), // input rd_en 
		.dout(val[(30+1)*`VEC_VAL_BITS-1:30*`VEC_VAL_BITS]), 
		.full(val_full[30]), // output full 
		.empty(val_empty[30]) // output empty 
	);

	generic_fifo_sc_a #( 
		.dw(`VEC_VAL_BITS), 
		.aw(FIFO_DEPTH_BITS) 
	) fifo_bvb_31 ( 
		.clk(clk), // input clk 
		.rst(rst), 
		.clr(1'b0), 
		.din(ram_out), 
		.we(val_wr_en[31]), // input wr_en 
		.re(val_rd_en[31]), // input rd_en 
		.dout(val[(31+1)*`VEC_VAL_BITS-1:31*`VEC_VAL_BITS]), 
		.full(val_full[31]), // output full 
		.empty(val_empty[31]) // output empty 
	);

	assign id_empty_shifted = id_empty >> counter;
	assign val_full_shifted = val_full >> counter;
	assign id_rd_en_local = (start & (~id_empty_shifted) & (~val_full_shifted));
	assign id_rd_en = id_rd_en_local << counter;

	always @ (posedge clk) begin
		if (rst) begin
			counter <= 0;
			counter_delay <= 0;
		end
		else if (start) begin
			counter <= counter + 1;
			counter_delay <= counter;
			id_rd_en_local_reg <= id_rd_en_local;
			val_wr_en <= id_rd_en_local_reg << counter_delay;
			rd_addr <= id >> (counter*`COL_ID_BITS);
		end
	end
endmodule

 "new_string": "\tif(re)\t\t\t\t\t\tgb <= 1'b0;\n\nendmodule\n\n// generic_fifo_sc_a_ar: identical to generic_fifo_sc_a in every control signal\n// (wp / rp / gb / empty / full), but with an asynchronously-read (combinational)\n// memory instead of the registered-read dpram. Its dout is therefore the same\n// function of the same FIFO state, one cycle earlier; the register that is\n// removed here is the operand register of the mac_int_18x19 block in Channel.\nmodule generic_fifo_sc_a_ar\n\t#(parameter dw=8,\n\t  parameter aw=8)\n\t(clk, rst, clr, din, we, dout, re,\n\tfull, empty);\n\nlocalparam max_size = 1<<aw;\n\ninput \t\t\tclk, rst, clr;\ninput \t[dw-1:0]\tdin;\ninput \t\t\twe;\noutput\t[dw-1:0]\tdout;\ninput \twire\t\tre;\noutput\t\t\tfull;\noutput\t\t\tempty;\n\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n//\n// Local Wires\n//\nreg\t\t[aw-1:0]\twp;\nwire\t[aw-1:0]\twp_pl1;\nreg\t\t[aw-1:0]\trp;\nwire\t[aw-1:0]\trp_pl1;\nreg\t\t\t\t\tgb;\n\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n//\n// Memory Block (write port registered, read port combinational)\n//\nreg\t[dw-1:0]\tmem [0:max_size-1];\n\nalways @(posedge clk)\n\tif(we)\t\tmem[wp] <= din;\n\nassign dout = mem[rp];\n\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n//\n// Misc Logic\n//\n\nalways @(posedge clk)\n\tif(rst)\twp <= {aw{1'b0}};\n\telse\n\tif(clr)\t\twp <= {aw{1'b0}};\n\telse\n\tif(we)\t\twp <= wp_pl1;\n\nassign wp_pl1 = wp + 1'b1;\n\nalways @(posedge clk)\n\tif(rst)\t\trp <= {aw{1'b0}};\n\telse\n\tif(clr)\t\trp <= {aw{1'b0}};\n\telse\n\tif(re)\t\trp <= rp_pl1;\n\nassign rp_pl1 = rp + 1'b1;\n\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n//\n// Combinatorial Full & Empty Flags\n//\n\nassign empty = ((wp == rp) & !gb);\nassign full  = ((wp == rp) &  gb);\n\n// Guard Bit ...\nalways @(posedge clk)\n\tif(rst)\t\t\t\t\t\tgb <= 1'b0;\n\telse\n\tif(clr)\t\t\t\t\t\tgb <= 1'b0;\n\telse\n\tif((wp_pl1 == rp) & we)\t\tgb <= 1'b1;\n\telse\n\tif(re)\t\t\t\t\t\tgb <= 1'b0;\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule dpram #("
